\displaystyle\,\left(\frac{\Delta}{\Sigma}2a\cos\theta-2ar\cos\theta(\tr\chi)+(r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta)(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot{\chi})\right)\bm{\rho}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{\Delta}{\Sigma}2r-2ar\cos\theta(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot{\chi})-(r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta)(\tr\chi)\right)\bm{\sigma}

\displaystyle+2ar\cos\theta(\divsl\bm{\alpha}+(\eta+\underline{\eta},\bm{\alpha}))-(r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta)(\curlsl\bm{\alpha}+(\eta+\underline{\eta})\wedge\bm{\alpha})

and

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{4}\widehat{\bm{\rho}}=

\displaystyle\,-\left(\frac{\Delta}{\Sigma}2a\cos\theta-2ar\cos\theta(\tr\chi)+(r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta)(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot{\chi})\right)\bm{\sigma}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{\Delta}{\Sigma}2r-2ar\cos\theta(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot{\chi})-(r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta)(\tr\chi)\right)\bm{\rho}

\displaystyle+2ar\cos\theta(\curlsl\bm{\alpha}+(\eta+\underline{\eta})\wedge\bm{\alpha})+(r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta)(\divsl\bm{\alpha}+(\eta+\underline{\eta},\bm{\alpha}))\,,

where we used the equations (
3.15
)–(
3.16
). To conclude, we use the fact that, in view of the identities of Section
2.2.4
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta}{\Sigma}2a\cos\theta-2ar\cos\theta(\tr\chi)+(r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta)(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot{\chi})

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta}{\Sigma}2r-2ar\cos\theta(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot{\chi})-(r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta)(\tr\chi)

\displaystyle=0\,.

We now derive the equations (
3.36
)–(
3.37
). By summing and subtracting the equations (
3.23
) and (
3.24
) (and taking horizontal Hodge duals), we obtain

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}(\Sigma\bm{\sigma})-\Sigma\bm{\rho}{}^{\star}(\eta-\underline{\eta})

\displaystyle=-{}^{\star}\mathfrak{T}(\bm{\alpha})-{}^{\star}\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\,,

(3.44)

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}(\Sigma\bm{\rho})+\Sigma\bm{\sigma}{}^{\star}(\eta-\underline{\eta})

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{T}(\bm{\alpha})-\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\,.

(3.45)

We then compute

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}

\displaystyle=\not{\nabla}(r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta)\Sigma\bm{\sigma}+\frac{r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}{\Sigma}\not{\nabla}(\Sigma\bm{\sigma})+(r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta)\not{\nabla}(\Sigma^{-1})\Sigma\bm{\sigma}

\displaystyle\qquad+\not{\nabla}(r\Sigma(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\underline{\chi}))\bm{\rho}+r(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\underline{\chi})\not{\nabla}(\Sigma\bm{\rho})+r(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\underline{\chi})\not{\nabla}(\Sigma^{-1})\Sigma\bm{\rho}

\displaystyle=(-(\eta+\underline{\eta})\Sigma-(r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta)(\eta+\underline{\eta})-r(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\underline{\chi}){}^{\star}(\eta-\underline{\eta})\Sigma)\bm{\sigma}

\displaystyle\qquad-\left(-\frac{r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}{\Sigma}{}^{\star}(\eta-\underline{\eta})\Sigma-\not{\nabla}(r\Sigma(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\underline{\chi}))+r(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\underline{\chi})\Sigma(\eta+\underline{\eta})\right)\bm{\rho}

\displaystyle\qquad+r(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\underline{\chi})(\mathfrak{T}(\bm{\alpha})-\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(\bm{\underline{\alpha}}))-\frac{r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}{\Sigma}({}^{\star}\mathfrak{T}(\bm{\alpha})+{}^{\star}\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(\bm{\underline{\alpha}}))

and

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}\widehat{\bm{\rho}}

\displaystyle=\not{\nabla}(r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta)\Sigma\bm{\rho}+\frac{r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}{\Sigma}\not{\nabla}(\Sigma\bm{\rho})+(r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta)\not{\nabla}(\Sigma^{-1})\Sigma\bm{\rho}

\displaystyle\qquad-\not{\nabla}(r\Sigma(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\underline{\chi}))\bm{\sigma}-r(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\underline{\chi})\not{\nabla}(\Sigma\bm{\sigma})-r(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\underline{\chi})\not{\nabla}(\Sigma^{-1})\Sigma\bm{\sigma}

\displaystyle=(-(\eta+\underline{\eta})\Sigma-(r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta)(\eta+\underline{\eta})-r(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\underline{\chi}){}^{\star}(\eta-\underline{\eta})\Sigma)\bm{\rho}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(-\frac{r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}{\Sigma}{}^{\star}(\eta-\underline{\eta})\Sigma-\not{\nabla}(r\Sigma(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\underline{\chi}))+r(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\underline{\chi})\Sigma(\eta+\underline{\eta})\right)\bm{\sigma}

\displaystyle\qquad+r(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\underline{\chi})({}^{\star}\mathfrak{T}(\bm{\alpha})+{}^{\star}\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(\bm{\underline{\alpha}}))+\frac{r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}{\Sigma}(\mathfrak{T}(\bm{\alpha})-\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(\bm{\underline{\alpha}}))\,,

where we used (
2.20
) and the identities (
3.44
)–(
3.45
). Both the brackets multiplying
\bm{\rho}
and
\bm{\sigma}
can be checked to identically vanish, i.e.

\displaystyle-(\eta+\underline{\eta})\Sigma-(r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta)(\eta+\underline{\eta})-r(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\underline{\chi}){}^{\star}(\eta-\underline{\eta})\Sigma

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle-(r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta){}^{\star}(\eta-\underline{\eta})-r\Sigma(\tr\underline{\chi}){}^{\star}\eta+r\Sigma(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\underline{\chi})\underline{\eta}

\displaystyle=0\,,

where we used the geometric relation (
2.21
) and the identities of Section
2.2.4
.
∎

3.2.3
Notation for solutions

In the sequel, it will be useful to have some notation to distinguish solutions to the original and modified Maxwell system. We let

\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}=(\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}],\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}],\bm{\rho}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}],\bm{\sigma}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}])

be a
solution
to the system of Maxwell equations (
3.13
)–(
3.18
). We denote by

\bm{\mathfrak{S}}=(\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}],\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}],\widehat{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}],\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}])

the corresponding
modified solution
(i.e. solution to the modified system of Maxwell equations (
3.32
)–(
3.37
)), with

\displaystyle\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]

\displaystyle=\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}]\,,

\displaystyle\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]

\displaystyle=\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}]\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]

\displaystyle=\widehat{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}]\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]

\displaystyle=\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}]\,.

3.3
Special solutions

In this section, we present two classes of special solutions to the system of Maxwell equations.

3.3.1
Stationary solutions

Let
\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, to be interpreted as electromagnetic charges. We define the two-parameter family of stationary (smooth) solutions
\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}{}_{\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}}
such that

\displaystyle\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}{}_{\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}}]

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}{}_{\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}}]

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\bm{\rho}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}{}_{\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Sigma}\left(\mathfrak{q}_{1}\frac{r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}{\Sigma}+2\,\mathfrak{q}_{2}\frac{ar\cos\theta}{\Sigma}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\bm{\sigma}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}{}_{\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Sigma}\left(-2\,\mathfrak{q}_{1}\frac{ar\cos\theta}{\Sigma}+\mathfrak{q}_{2}\frac{r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}{\Sigma}\right)\,,

which induces the two-parameter family of modified stationary (smooth) solutions
\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}}
such that

\displaystyle\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}}]

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}}]

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}}]

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{q}_{1}\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}}]

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{q}_{2}\,.

Using the modified system of Maxwell equations (
3.32
)–(
3.37
), it is immediate to check that, if
\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]=\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]=0
for a given modified solution
\bm{\mathfrak{S}}
, then
\bm{\mathfrak{S}}
is a modified stationary solution.

3.3.2
Solutions supported on a fixed azimuthal mode

Let
m\in\mathbb{\mathbb{Z}}
. We say that a solution
\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}
of the system of Maxwell equations is supported on the fixed azimuthal mode
m
if

\displaystyle\widetilde{Z}\big(\upalpha^{[{\pm 1}]}\big)

\displaystyle=im\,\upalpha^{[{\pm 1}]},

where
\Delta^{\pm 1}\upalpha^{[{\pm 1}]}\in\mathscr{S}^{[\pm 1]}_{\infty}(\mathcal{M})
are the image of
\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}]
and
\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}]
, respectively, under the invertible maps
\mathrm{i}^{[\pm 1]}
(see Lemma
3.2
), and if

\displaystyle\widetilde{Z}\big(\bm{\rho}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}]\big)

\displaystyle=im\,\bm{\rho}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}]\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{Z}\big(\bm{\sigma}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}]\big)

\displaystyle=im\,\bm{\sigma}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}],

after promoting
(\bm{\rho}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}],\bm{\sigma}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}])
to complex-valued functions on
\mathcal{M}
. This induces a modified solution
\bm{\mathfrak{S}}
supported on the fixed azimuthal mode
m
, which is such that

\displaystyle\widetilde{Z}\big(\upalpha^{[{\pm 1}]}\big)

\displaystyle=im\,\upalpha^{[{\pm 1}]}\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{Z}\big(\bm{\rho}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big)

\displaystyle=im\,\bm{\rho}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{Z}\big(\bm{\sigma}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big)

\displaystyle=im\,\bm{\sigma}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}],

where
\Delta^{\pm 1}\upalpha^{[{\pm 1}]}\in\mathscr{S}^{[\pm 1]}_{\infty}(\mathcal{M})
are the image of
\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]=\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}]
and
\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]=\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}]
, respectively, under the invertible maps
\mathrm{i}^{[\pm 1]}
.

3.4
Initial data and well-posedness

The Maxwell equations (
3.13
)–(
3.18
) form an overdetermined system of equations. As a result, the initial data for the system have to satisfy a set of constraint equations. In this section, we discuss how initial data are to be prescribed and briefly comment on the (global) well-posedness of the Maxwell equations (
3.13
)–(
3.18
). In fact, it will be convenient to present our discussion in terms of the modified Maxwell equations (
3.32
)–(
3.37
) and modified solutions
\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}
. Through the identities (
3.29
)–(
3.30
), our statements will immediately imply analogous statements for the original Maxwell equations (
3.13
)–(
3.18
) and solutions
\bm{\mathfrak{S}}
.

In this section, as well as in the remainder of the paper, we always consider smooth solutions to the Maxwell equations.

3.4.1
Seed initial data

The initial data for the modified Maxwell equations (
3.32
)–(
3.37
) are prescribed on the hyperboloidal hypersurface
\Sigma_{0}
(recall definition (
2.9
)). The initial data quantities which can be freely prescribed will be referred to as
seed initial data
. Since
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}\not\subset T\Sigma_{0}
, formulating a notion of seed initial data for a system of equations for
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
tensors, starting from a suitable prescription of quantities on (and intrinsic to)
\Sigma_{0}
, is slightly subtle.

As a preliminary step, it is useful to relate
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
one-forms to spacetime one-forms. The former admit canonical extensions to smooth sections

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\circ}((T\mathcal{M})^{\star})

\displaystyle:=\{\bm{\varsigma}\in\Gamma((T\mathcal{M})^{\star})\colon\bm{\varsigma}(e_{3}^{\text{as}})=\bm{\varsigma}(e_{4}^{\text{as}})=0\}\,,

i.e. spacetime one-forms which identically vanish when evaluated on
e_{3}^{\text{as}}
and
e_{4}^{\text{as}}
(see e.g.
[
10
, Section 7.2]
). By employing the hyperboloidal differentiable structure of Section
2.1.2
, a more explicit characterization of the canonical extension of
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
one-forms is as follows:

Lemma 3.5
.

There exist smooth scalar functions
c_{1,2}(r)
such that the following holds true:
\bm{\varsigma}\in\Gamma_{\circ}((T\mathcal{M})^{\star})
if and only if there exists
\bm{\varsigma}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tilde{t}{}^{*},r}}\in\Gamma((T\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tilde{t}{}^{*},r})^{\star})
such that

\bm{\varsigma}=[c_{1}(r)\,\bm{\varsigma}{}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tilde{t}{}^{*},r}}(Z)]\,d\tilde{t}{}^{*}+[c_{2}(r)\,\bm{\varsigma}{}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tilde{t}{}^{*},r}}(Z)]\,dr+\bm{\varsigma}{}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tilde{t}{}^{*},r}}\,,

and therefore there exists an isomorphism between
\Gamma_{\circ}((T\mathcal{M})^{\star})
and
\Gamma((T\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tilde{t}{}^{*},r})^{\star})
.

Proof.

For any
\bm{\varsigma}\in\Gamma_{\circ}((T\mathcal{M})^{\star})
, we have

\displaystyle\bm{\varsigma}=\bm{\varsigma}(\partial_{\tilde{t}{}^{*}})d\tilde{t}{}^{*}+\bm{\varsigma}(\partial_{r})dr+\bm{\varsigma}(\partial_{\theta})d\theta+\bm{\varsigma}(\partial_{\tilde{\phi}{}^{*}})d\tilde{\phi}{}^{*}\,.

The conditions
\bm{\varsigma}(e_{4}^{\text{as}})=\bm{\varsigma}(e_{3}^{\text{as}})=0
are equivalent to the constraining identities

\displaystyle\bm{\varsigma}(\partial_{\tilde{t}{}^{*}})

\displaystyle=c_{1}(r)\,\bm{\varsigma}(\partial_{\tilde{\phi}{}^{*}})\,,

\displaystyle\bm{\varsigma}(\partial_{r})

\displaystyle=c_{2}(r)\,\bm{\varsigma}(\partial_{\tilde{\phi}{}^{*}})

for some smooth scalar functions
c_{1,2}(r)
. By defining

\bm{\varsigma}{}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tilde{t}{}^{*},r}}=\bm{\varsigma}(\partial_{\theta})d\theta+\bm{\varsigma}(\partial_{\tilde{\phi}{}^{*}})d\tilde{\phi}{}^{*}\,,

one can write
\bm{\varsigma}
as in the lemma.
∎

Remark 3.6
.

One has
c_{1,2}(r)\rightarrow 0
as
r\rightarrow\infty
and
c_{1,2}(r)
identically vanishing for
|a|=0
.

We now state the definition of seed initial data for the modified Maxwell equations (
3.32
)–(
3.37
). We recall that, by standard Hodge decomposition on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
, any
\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tilde{t}{}^{*},r}
one-form
\bm{\varsigma}{}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tilde{t}{}^{*},r}}
is uniquely represented as

\bm{\varsigma}{}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tilde{t}{}^{*},r}}=-\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tilde{t}{}^{*},r}}\bm{f}+{}^{\star}\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tilde{t}{}^{*},r}}\bm{h}\,,

with
\bm{f}
and
\bm{h}
smooth scalar functions.

Definition 3.7

(Seed initial data)
.

Let

(\mathfrak{f},\underline{\mathfrak{f}},\mathfrak{h},\underline{\mathfrak{h}})

(3.46)

be smooth scalar functions on
\Sigma_{0}
and

(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{s})

(3.47)

be smooth scalar functions on
\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}}=\Sigma_{0}\cap\mathcal{H}^{+}
. We refer to the scalars (
3.46
)–(
3.47
) as
seed quantities
on
\Sigma_{0}
. We define
seed initial data
on
\Sigma_{0}
for the modified Maxwell equations (
3.32
)–(
3.37
) as the (canonical extensions of)
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
one-forms

\begin{split}\bm{\alpha}|_{\Sigma_{0}}=&\,[c_{1}(r)\cdot\,(-\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r}}\mathfrak{f}+{}^{\star}\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r}}\mathfrak{h})(Z)]\,d\tilde{t}{}^{*}+[c_{2}(r)\cdot\,(-\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r}}\mathfrak{f}+{}^{\star}\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r}}\mathfrak{h})(Z)]\,dr\\
&-\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r}}\mathfrak{f}+{}^{\star}\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r}}\mathfrak{h}\,,\\
\bm{\underline{\alpha}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}=&\,[c_{1}(r)\cdot\,(-\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r}}\underline{\mathfrak{f}}+{}^{\star}\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r}}\underline{\mathfrak{h}})(Z)]\,d\tilde{t}{}^{*}+[c_{2}(r)\cdot\,(-\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r}}\underline{\mathfrak{f}}+{}^{\star}\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r}}\underline{\mathfrak{h}})(Z)]\,dr\\
&-\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r}}\underline{\mathfrak{f}}+{}^{\star}\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r}}\underline{\mathfrak{h}}\end{split}

(3.48)

on
\Sigma_{0}
and smooth scalar functions

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{\rho}}\,|_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}}}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{r}\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}\,|_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}}}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{s}

(3.49)

on the horizon sphere
\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}}
.

Remark 3.8
.

The family of modified stationary solutions arises from seed quantities

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{f},\underline{\mathfrak{f}},\mathfrak{h},\underline{\mathfrak{h}})

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{s})

\displaystyle=(\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2})

on
\Sigma_{0}
and
\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}}
respectively.

Remark 3.9
.

Modified solutions supported on a fixed azimuthal number
m\in\mathbb{Z}
arise from seed quantities satisfying

\displaystyle Z(\mathfrak{f},\underline{\mathfrak{f}},\mathfrak{h},\underline{\mathfrak{h}})

\displaystyle=im\,(\mathfrak{f},\underline{\mathfrak{f}},\mathfrak{h},\underline{\mathfrak{h}})\,,

\displaystyle Z(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{s})

\displaystyle=im\,(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{s})\,.

on
\Sigma_{0}
and
\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}}
respectively. We refer to these as
seed initial data supported on the fixed azimuthal number

m\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Remark 3.10
.

In view of Lemma
3.5
, the space of seed quantities on
\Sigma_{0}
is isomorphic to the space of seed initial data on
\Sigma_{0}
.

3.4.2
Well-posedness

We have the following well-posedness statement for the modified Maxwell equations (
3.32
)–(
3.37
).

Proposition 3.11

(Well-posedness)
.

For any suitable
11
11

11

The proof of the proposition relies on solving a constraint (transport) equation along
\Sigma_{0}
. The seed initial data are to be prescribed so that this equation admits a global solution. For the main results of this work, we consider initial data whose finite energy norm implies that the constraint (transport) equation can indeed be integrated
globally
along
\Sigma_{0}
.
seed initial data on
\Sigma_{0}
, there exists a unique smooth solution to the modified Maxwell equations (
3.32
)–(
3.37
) on
J^{+}(\Sigma_{0})\cap\mathcal{M}
which agrees with the seed initial data on
\Sigma_{0}
.

Proof.

From any prescription of seed initial data on
\Sigma_{0}
, one can determine all the unknowns of the modified Maxwell equations (
3.32
)–(
3.37
) along
\Sigma_{0}
. This fact is more easily seen by exploiting the Maxwell equations (
3.40
)–(
3.43
). Indeed, by taking the linear combination

\displaystyle ia\sin\theta\frac{\Delta\overline{\kappa}^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}\kappa}\overline{\kappa}\cdot\eqref{eq:Maxwell-m}+ia\sin\theta\frac{\Delta\overline{\kappa}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\kappa\cdot\eqref{eq:Maxwell-m-bar}+\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\cdot\eqref{eq:Maxwell-l}-\frac{2\Sigma}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\cdot\eqref{eq:Maxwell-n}

one can eliminate the
\partial_{\tilde{t}^{*}}
-derivatives of all the unknowns and obtain a first order ODE for
\upupsilon^{[{0}]}
in a direction tangent to
\Sigma_{0}
(and transversal to the spheres foliating
\Sigma_{0}
). The seed initial data for
\bm{\widehat{\rho}}
and
\bm{\widehat{\sigma}}
prescribed on
\mathcal{H}^{+}\cap\Sigma_{0}
provides the initial datum for the ODE, whereas the seed initial data for
\bm{\alpha}
and
\bm{\underline{\alpha}}
prescribed on
\Sigma_{0}
completely determine the right hand side of the ODE on the entire
\Sigma_{0}
. The ODE can then be integrated along
\Sigma_{0}
so as to uniquely determine smooth
\upupsilon^{[{0}]}
(and therefore
\bm{\widehat{\rho}}
and
\bm{\widehat{\sigma}}
) on the entire
\Sigma_{0}
.

Once all the unknowns are determined on
\Sigma_{0}
, the modified Maxwell equations (
3.32
)–(
3.37
) determine the first order normal (to
\Sigma_{0}
) covariant derivative of all the unknowns along
\Sigma_{0}
.

The fact that
\bm{\alpha}
and
\bm{\underline{\alpha}}
decouple into Teukolsky equations, together with the standard well-posedness theory (in the smooth class) for the Cauchy problem for the Teukolsky equations (in light of Lemma
3.2
, see e.g.
[
35
, Proposition 4.12]
), guarantee that one can then uniquely determine smooth
\bm{\alpha}
and
\bm{\underline{\alpha}}
on
J^{+}(\Sigma_{0})\cap\mathcal{M}
. Knowing
\bm{\alpha}
and
\bm{\underline{\alpha}}
on
J^{+}(\Sigma_{0})\cap\mathcal{M}
, one applies the equations to uniquely determine smooth
\widehat{\bm{\rho}}
and
\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}
on
J^{+}(\Sigma_{0})\cap\mathcal{M}
. One can check that the first order ODE originally used to determine
\bm{\widehat{\rho}}
and
\bm{\widehat{\sigma}}
on
\Sigma_{0}
is automatically satisfied on any
\Sigma_{\tau}\subset J^{+}(\Sigma_{0})\cap\mathcal{M}
with
\tau\geq 0
(i.e. propagation of constraints).
∎

4
Energy norms

In this section, we define energy norms for both extremal and middle Maxwell components. The energy norms are defined over the hyperboloidal hypersurfaces
\Sigma_{\tau}
defined in (
2.9
) (see also Figure (
1
)).

4.1
Extremal Maxwell components

Let
\bm{\alpha}
and
\bm{\underline{\alpha}}
be
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\rm as}}
one-forms, and let
p\in[0,2)
. We define the second order energy fluxes

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{p}^{1}[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}](\tau)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left(r^{p}\big|\not{\nabla}^{2}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})\big|^{2}+r^{p}\big|{\not{\nabla}}\not{\nabla}_{4}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})\big|^{2}+r^{p}\big|\not{\nabla}_{4}^{2}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})\big|^{2}\right)drd\Omega

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left(r^{p+2}\big|\not{\nabla}_{4}\not{\nabla}_{3}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})\big|^{2}+\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}}\big|\not{\nabla}_{3}^{2}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})\big|^{2}+r^{2}\big|{\not{\nabla}}\not{\nabla}_{3}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})\big|^{2}+\big|\not{\nabla}_{3}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})\big|^{2}\right)drd\Omega

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left(r^{p}\big|\not{\nabla}_{4}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})\big|^{2}+\big|{\not{\nabla}}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})\big|^{2}+r^{-2}\big|r^{2}\bm{\alpha}\big|^{2}\right)drd\Omega\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{1}[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}](\tau)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left(r^{p}\big|{\not{\nabla}}^{2}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})\big|^{2}+r^{p}\big|{\not{\nabla}}\not{\nabla}_{4}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})\big|^{2}+r^{p}\big|\not{\nabla}_{4}^{2}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})\big|^{2}\right)drd\Omega

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left(r^{p+2}\big|\not{\nabla}_{4}\not{\nabla}_{3}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})\big|^{2}+\big|\not{\nabla}_{3}^{2}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})\big|^{2}+r^{2}\big|{\not{\nabla}}\not{\nabla}_{3}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})\big|^{2}+\big|\not{\nabla}_{3}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})\big|^{2}\right)drd\Omega

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left(r^{p}\big|\not{\nabla}_{4}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})\big|^{2}+\big|\not{\nabla}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})\big|^{2}+r^{-2}\big|r^{2}\bm{\alpha}\big|^{2}\right)drd\Omega\,,

for
\bm{\alpha}
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{p}^{1}[r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}](\tau)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}}\left(r^{p}\big|{\not{\nabla}}^{2}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\big|^{2}+r^{-2}(r-M)^{2}\big|\not{\nabla}_{3}{\not{\nabla}}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\big|^{2}+r^{-6}(r-M)^{4}\big|\not{\nabla}_{3}^{2}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\big|^{2}\right)drd\Omega

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left(r^{p}\big|\not{\nabla}_{4}\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\big|^{2}+\frac{\Delta}{r^{4}}\big|\not{\nabla}_{3}\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\big|^{2}+\big|{\not{\nabla}}\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\big|^{2}+r^{-2}\big|\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\big|^{2}\right)drd\Omega

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left(r^{-6}(r-M)^{2}\Delta\big|\not{\nabla}_{3}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\big|^{2}+r^{-2}(r-M)^{2}\left(\big|{\not{\nabla}}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\big|^{2}+r^{-2}\big|r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}\big|^{2}\right)\right)drd\Omega\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{1}[r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}](\tau)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left(r^{p}\big|{\not{\nabla}}^{2}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\big|^{2}+r^{-1}\big|{\not{\nabla}}\not{\nabla}_{3}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\big|^{2}+r^{-2}\big|\not{\nabla}_{3}^{2}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\big|^{2}\right)drd\Omega

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left(r^{p+4}\big|\not{\nabla}_{4}^{2}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\big|^{2}+\big|\not{\nabla}_{3}\not{\nabla}_{4}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\big|^{2}+r^{4}\big|{\not{\nabla}}\not{\nabla}_{4}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\big|^{2}+r^{2}\big|\not{\nabla}_{4}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\big|^{2}\right)drd\Omega

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left(r^{-2}\big|\not{\nabla}_{3}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\big|^{2}+\big|{\not{\nabla}}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\big|^{2}+r^{-2}\big|r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}\big|^{2}\right)drd\Omega

for
\bm{\underline{\alpha}}
(recall definitions (
3.38
)). We note that what distinguishes
\mathbb{E}_{p}
from
\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{p}
is the degeneracy of the former norms at the event horizon (i.e. at
r=r_{+}
). We will also consider corresponding higher order energy fluxes

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{J+1}_{p}[\bm{\varsigma}](\tau)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}\leq J}\mathbb{E}_{p}^{1}\left[\left((1-Mr^{-1})\not{\nabla}_{3}\right)^{J_{1}}\not{\nabla}_{4}^{J_{2}}\not{\nabla}^{J_{3}}\bm{\varsigma}\right](\tau)\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{J+1}_{p}[\bm{\varsigma}](\tau)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}\leq J}\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{1}\left[\not{\nabla}_{3}^{J_{1}}\not{\nabla}_{4}^{J_{2}}\not{\nabla}^{J_{3}}\bm{\varsigma}\right](\tau)

for
J\geq 0
and
\bm{\varsigma}=\{r^{2}\bm{\alpha},r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}\}
. By convention, we write
{\mathbb{E}}^{J+1}={\mathbb{E}}^{J+1}_{0}
and
\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{J+1}=\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{J+1}_{0}
. Finally, it will be useful to introduce notation for the degenerate (first order) energy flux of
\bm{\alpha}
through
\mathcal{H}^{+}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}](\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tilde{t}{}^{*},r_{+}}}\left(|r^{2}\bm{\alpha}|^{2}+|{\not{\nabla}}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{4}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})|^{2}\right)d\Omega d\tilde{t}{}^{*}

for any
\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}\leq\infty
, and for the non-degenerate (second order) energy fluxes of
\bm{\alpha}
and
\bm{\underline{\alpha}}
through
\mathcal{H}^{+}
,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{1}_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}](\tau_{1},\tau_{2})

\displaystyle=\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tilde{t}{}^{*},r_{+}}}\left(|\not{\nabla}{\mathfrak{T}}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{4}{\mathfrak{T}}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})|^{2}+|{\mathfrak{T}}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})|^{2}\right)d\Omega d\tilde{t}{}^{*}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tilde{t}{}^{*},r_{+}}}\left(|\not{\nabla}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{4}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})|^{2}+|r^{2}\bm{\alpha}|^{2}\right)d\Omega d\tilde{t}{}^{*},

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{1}_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}[r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}](\tau_{1},\tau_{2})

\displaystyle=\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tilde{t}{}^{*},r_{+}}}\left(|\not{\nabla}\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{4}\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})|^{2}+|\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})|^{2}\right)d\Omega d\tilde{t}{}^{*}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tilde{t}{}^{*},r_{+}}}\left(|\not{\nabla}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{4}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})|^{2}+|r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}|^{2}\right)d\Omega d\tilde{t}{}^{*}

for any
\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}\leq\infty
.

4.1.1
Relation with spin-weighted energy norms

In the
|a|<M
case, the energy norms above can be compared to those introduced in
[
35
]
for spin-weighted quantities.

Let
\upalpha^{[{\pm 1}]}
be the
(\pm 1)-
spin-weighted functions obtained from
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\underline{\alpha}})
through the isomorphism of Lemma
3.2
. We define

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\upphi^{[{\pm 1}]}_{0}=\frac{\Delta^{\frac{1\pm 1}{2}}}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\upalpha^{[{\pm 1}]}\,,\end{gathered}

the rescalings

\displaystyle\tilde{\upphi}^{[{+1}]}_{0}

\displaystyle:=\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\upphi^{[{+1}]}_{0}=\Delta^{+1}\upalpha^{[{+1}]}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\Sigma\bm{\alpha}(e_{1}^{\rm as}+ie_{2}^{\rm as})\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{\upphi}^{[{-1}]}_{0}

\displaystyle:=\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}\upphi^{[{-1}]}_{0}=\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Delta^{-1}\upalpha^{[{-1}]}=\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\sqrt{2}\Sigma}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{1/2}\bm{\underline{\alpha}}(e_{1}^{\rm as}-ie_{2}^{\rm as})

and the so-called
transformed
quantities

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Phi^{[{+1}]}&:=(r^{2}+a^{2})e_{3}^{\rm as}(\upphi^{[{+1}]}_{0})=\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{1/2}}{\sqrt{2}}\left[-\mathfrak{T}(\bm{\alpha})+\left(\frac{a^{2}r\sin^{2}\theta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}-ia\cos\theta\right)\bm{\alpha}\right](e_{1}^{\rm as}+ie_{2}^{\rm as})\,,\\
\Phi^{[{-1}]}&:=\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}\Sigma e_{4}^{\rm as}(\upphi^{[{-1}]}_{0})=\frac{\kappa^{2}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{1/2}}{\sqrt{2}\Sigma}\left[\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(\bm{\underline{\alpha}})+\frac{\Delta}{\Sigma}\left(\frac{a^{2}r\sin^{2}\theta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}+ia\cos\theta\right)\bm{\underline{\alpha}}\right](e_{1}^{\rm as}-ie_{2}^{\rm as})\,.\end{split}

(4.2)

By applying Lemma
3.2
, one can obtain pointwise estimates (whose implicit constants depend only on
M
) relating the absolute value of the above complex scalars with the pointwise norms of the tensorial quantities
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\underline{\alpha}})
. For instance, from the definitions (
3.28
), one can compute

\displaystyle\big|\tilde{\upphi}^{[{+1}]}_{0}\big|^{2}

\displaystyle=|\Delta\upalpha^{[{+1}]}|^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\lvert\Sigma\bm{\alpha}\rvert_{{\not{g}}}^{2},

\displaystyle\big|\tilde{\upphi}^{[{-1}]}_{0}\big|^{2}

\displaystyle=|(r^{2}+a^{2})^{1/2}\Delta^{-1}\upalpha^{[{-1}]}|^{2}=\frac{1}{2}|\kappa^{-2}\Sigma|^{2}\,\lvert(r^{2}+a^{2})^{1/2}\bm{\underline{\alpha}}\rvert_{{\not{g}}}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\lvert(r^{2}+a^{2})^{1/2}\bm{\underline{\alpha}}\rvert_{{\not{g}}}^{2},

\displaystyle\big|\Phi^{[{-1}]}\big|^{2}

\displaystyle\sim|\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})|_{{\not{g}}}^{2}+\frac{\Delta}{r^{4}}|r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}|_{{\not{g}}}^{2}

and other similar relations. We note that the computations leading to such relations make use of the particular choice of frame vectors (
2.13
), but eventually yield relations which are independent of any such choice.

With the above relations in mind, the degenerate energy norms introduced in
[
35
]
can be shown to satisfy (recall definition (
2.14
))

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{p}\big[\tilde{\upphi}_{0}^{[{+1}]}\big](\tau)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left(r^{p}\big|L\tilde{\upphi}_{0}^{[{+1}]}\big|^{2}+\frac{1}{\Delta}\big|\underline{L}\tilde{\upphi}_{0}^{[{+1}]}\big|^{2}+r^{-2}\big|\mathring{\not{\nabla}}\tilde{\upphi}_{0}^{[{+1}]}\big|^{2}+r^{-2}\big|\tilde{\upphi}_{0}^{[{+1}]}\big|^{2}\right)drd\Omega

\displaystyle\sim\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left(r^{p}\big|\not{\nabla}_{4}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})\big|^{2}+\frac{\Delta}{r^{4}}\big|\not{\nabla}_{3}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})\big|^{2}+\big|{\not{\nabla}}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})\big|^{2}+r^{-2}\big|r^{2}\bm{\alpha}\big|^{2}\right)drd\Omega\,,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{p}\big[\Phi^{[{+1}]}\big](\tau)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left(r^{p}\big|L\Phi^{[{+1}]}\big|^{2}+\frac{1}{\Delta}\big|\underline{L}\Phi^{[{+1}]}\big|^{2}+r^{-2}\big|\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{[+1]}\Phi^{[{+1}]}\big|^{2}+r^{-2}\big|\Phi^{[{+1}]}\big|^{2}\right)drd\Omega

\displaystyle\sim\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left(r^{p+2}\big|\not{\nabla}_{4}\not{\nabla}_{3}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})\big|^{2}+\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}}\big|\not{\nabla}_{3}^{2}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})\big|^{2}+r^{2}\big|{\not{\nabla}}\not{\nabla}_{3}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})\big|^{2}+\big|\not{\nabla}_{3}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})\big|^{2}\right)drd\Omega

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}_{p}\big[\tilde{\upphi}_{0}^{[{+1}]}\big](\tau)

for spin
+1
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{p}\big[\tilde{\upphi}_{0}^{[{-1}]}\big](\tau)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left(r^{p}\big|L\tilde{\upphi}^{[{-1}]}_{0}\big|^{2}+\frac{1}{\Delta}\big|\underline{L}\tilde{\upphi}_{0}^{[{-1}]}\big|^{2}+r^{-2}\big|\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{[-1]}\tilde{\upphi}_{0}^{[{-1}]}\big|^{2}+r^{-2}\big|\tilde{\upphi}_{0}^{[{-1}]}\big|^{2}\right)drd\Omega

\displaystyle\sim\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left(r^{p}\big|\not{\nabla}_{4}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\big|^{2}+\frac{\Delta}{r^{4}}\big|\not{\nabla}_{3}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\big|^{2}+\big|{\not{\nabla}}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\big|^{2}+r^{-2}\big|r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}\big|^{2}\right)drd\Omega\,,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{p}\big[\Phi^{[{-1}]}\big](\tau)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left(r^{p}\big|L\Phi^{[{-1}]}\big|^{2}+\frac{1}{\Delta}\big|\underline{L}\Phi^{[{-1}]}\big|^{2}+r^{-2}\big|\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{[-1]}\Phi^{[{-1}]}\big|^{2}+r^{-2}\big|\Phi^{[{-1}]}\big|^{2}\right)drd\Omega

\displaystyle\sim\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left(r^{p}\big|\not{\nabla}_{4}\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\big|^{2}+\frac{\Delta}{r^{4}}\big|\not{\nabla}_{3}\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\big|^{2}+\big|{\not{\nabla}}\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\big|^{2}+r^{-2}\big|\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})\big|^{2}\right)drd\Omega

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}_{p}\big[\tilde{\upphi}_{0}^{[{-1}]}\big](\tau)

for spin
-1
. Here,
\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{[\pm 1]}
is the spinorial gradient defined in e.g.
[
35
, Section 3]
. By adding up, for each spin sign, the two energy norms, one obtains almost every term in the energy norms introduced in Section
4.1
for the tensorial quantities
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\underline{\alpha}})
, i.e. almost every term in
\mathbb{E}_{p}[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}]
and
\mathbb{E}_{p}[r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}]
. The missing terms are obtained by elliptic estimates, as explained in
[
35
, Section 4]
. To obtain the non-degenerate energy norms
\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{p}[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}]
and
\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{p}[r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}]
, one follows a similar procedure: one considers the spin-weighted energy norms
\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{p}[\tilde{\upphi}^{[{\pm 1}]}]
and
\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{p}[\Phi^{[{\pm 1}]}]
in
[
35
]
, and then revisit the elliptic estimates there accordingly.

Remark 4.1
.

The reader familiar with the standard definitions of energy norms for wave equations may be surprised to find zeroth order terms in the energy norms above. However, we recall from
[
35
, Section 3]
that, by the properties of the spinorial gradient, one has

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left(r^{-2}\big|\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{[s]}\Phi^{[{s}]}\big|^{2}+\frac{r-M}{r^{4}}\big|\Phi^{[{s}]}\big|^{2}\right)drd\Omega\gtrsim\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left(r^{-2}\big|\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{[s]}\Phi^{[{s}]}\big|^{2}+r^{-2}\left(s^{2}+\frac{r-M}{r^{2}}\right)\big|\Phi^{[{s}]}\big|^{2}\right)drd\Omega

for any
s
-spin-weighted function
\Phi^{[{s}]}
, with
s\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}
.

4.2
Middle Maxwell components

Let
\bm{f}=\left\{\widehat{\bm{\rho}},\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}\right\}
. We define the first order energy fluxes

\displaystyle\dot{\mathbb{E}}[\bm{f}](\tau)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left[|\not{\nabla}_{4}\bm{f}|^{2}+\Delta r^{-4}|\not{\nabla}_{3}\bm{f}|^{2}+|{\not{\nabla}}\bm{f}|^{2}\right]drd\Omega\,,

\displaystyle\dot{\overline{\mathbb{E}}}[\bm{f}](\tau)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left[|\not{\nabla}_{4}\bm{f}|^{2}+r^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{3}{\bm{f}}\,|^{2}+|{\not{\nabla}}\bm{f}|^{2}\right]drd\Omega\,,

where the overline indicates the non-degeneracy of the energy norm at the event horizon (i.e. at
r=r_{+}
), and corresponding higher order energy fluxes

\displaystyle\dot{\mathbb{E}}^{J}[\bm{f}](\tau)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}\leq J}\dot{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left((1-Mr^{-1})\not{\nabla}_{3}\right)^{J_{1}}\not{\nabla}_{4}^{J_{2}}\not{\nabla}^{J_{3}}\bm{f}\right](\tau)\,,

\displaystyle\dot{\overline{\mathbb{E}}}^{J}[\bm{f}](\tau)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}\leq J}\dot{\overline{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\not{\nabla}_{3}^{J_{1}}\not{\nabla}_{4}^{J_{2}}\not{\nabla}^{J_{3}}\bm{f}\right](\tau)

for
J\geq 0
. We note that
\dot{\mathbb{E}}^{0}=\dot{\mathbb{E}}
and
\dot{\overline{\mathbb{E}}}^{0}=\dot{\overline{\mathbb{E}}}
. The dot indicates that the energy norm does
not
contain a zeroth order term. It will also be convenient to define the energy fluxes

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{J}[\bm{f}](\tau)

\displaystyle:=\dot{\mathbb{E}}^{J}[\bm{f}](\tau)+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-2}|\bm{f}|^{2}drd\Omega\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{J}[\bm{f}](\tau)

\displaystyle:=\dot{\overline{\mathbb{E}}}^{J}[\bm{f}](\tau)+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-2}|\bm{f}|^{2}drd\Omega\,,

which contain a zeroth order term.

5
Statement of the main theorems

In this section, we provide a precise statement of our main theorems. All results are stated for the modified Maxwell equations (
3.32
)–(
3.37
). The reader can easily retrieve analogous statements for the original Maxwell equations (
3.13
)–(
3.18
).

The first theorem proven in the present paper concerns energy boundedness and decay for the Maxwell components in the sub-extremal
|a|<M
case. As we shall explain in Section
7
, the energy boundedness and decay statements for the
extremal
Maxwell components stated in Theorem
5.1
are a direct translation of the results obtained in
[
34
,
35
]
.

Theorem 5.1

(Sub-extremal case)
.

Fix
M>0
and
a_{0}\in[0,M)
. Let
J\geq 1
and
\eta\in(0,1)
. Then, there exists a uniform constant
C=C(a_{0},M,J,\eta)>0
such that, for any
|a|\leq a_{0}
and any solution

\bm{\mathfrak{S}}=(\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}],\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}],\widehat{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}],\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}])

to the modified Maxwell equations (
3.32
)–(
3.37
) arising from seed initial data prescribed on
\Sigma_{0}
, the following energy estimates hold.

•

Non-degenerate energy boundedness: if
J\geq 3
, then

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{J}\big[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](\tau)

\displaystyle\leq C\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{J}\big[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{J}\big[r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](\tau)

\displaystyle\leq C\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{J}\big[r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{J}\big[\widehat{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](\tau)+\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{J}\big[\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](\tau)

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\lVert|\widehat{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}})}+\lVert\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}})}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+C\left(\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{2-\eta}^{3}\big[r^{2}{\bm{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)+\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{J}\big[r^{2}{\bm{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)+\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{J}\big[r{\bm{\underline{\alpha}}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)\right)

for all
\tau\geq 0
.

•

Non-degenerate energy decay (extremal Maxwell components): we have

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{J}\big[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](\tau)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{(1+\tau)^{2-\eta}}\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{2-\eta}^{J+2}\big[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{J}\big[r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](\tau)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{(1+\tau)^{2-\eta}}\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{J+2}_{2-\eta}\big[r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)

for all
\tau\geq 0
.

•

Non-degenerate energy decay (middle Maxwell components): there exist finite
\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, which can be read off from the seed initial data, such that the normalised solution
\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}=\bm{\mathfrak{S}}-\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\bm{\alpha}[\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}](\tau)

\displaystyle=\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}](\tau)\,,

\displaystyle\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}](\tau)

\displaystyle=\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}](\tau)

and

\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{J}\big[\widehat{\bm{\rho}}\big[\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}\big]\big](\tau)+\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{J}\big[\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}\big[\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}\big]\big](\tau)\leq\frac{C}{(1+\tau)^{1-\eta}}\left(\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{2-\eta}^{J+3}\big[r^{2}{\bm{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)+\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{2-\eta}^{J+3}\big[r{\bm{\underline{\alpha}}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)\right)

for all
\tau\geq 0
.

Remark 5.2
.

The energy norms appearing on the left hand side of the energy estimates in Theorem
5.1
are non-degenerate at the event horizon (i.e. at
r=r_{+}
).

Remark 5.3
.

In Theorem
5.1
, the energy boundedness statement does not lose derivatives. In that sense, it provides a true orbital stability statement, comparable to those obtained for
|a|\ll M
in
[
2
,
11
,
33
]
.

Remark 5.4
.

The energy decay statement of Theorem
5.1
can be improved, provided that higher regularity and stronger
r
-decay are assumed on the initial data. Sharp decay estimates in this form are obtained in
[
28
]
.

Remark 5.5
.

We expect the loss in
r
-weights of Theorem
5.1
to be suboptimal. Treating the middle Maxwell components as solutions to a wave equation (see Appendix
A.3
) would lead to improved
r
-weights.

In Theorem
5.1
, we have
C(a_{0})\to\infty
as
a_{0}\to M
, and thus the energy boundedness and decay statements break down towards extremality. As we shall explain in Section
7
, such a breakdown emerges from the breakdown of energy boundedness and decay statements for the
extremal
Maxwell components. Nevertheless, it is expected that energy boundedness and (weaker) decay for the extremal Maxwell components still hold in the nearly extremal
|a|\approx M
case, at least under some additional assumptions on the initial data. More precisely, one may conjecture:

Conjecture 5.6

(Sub-extremal and extremal cases)
.

Fix
M>0
. Let
\eta\in(0,1)
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}
. Then, there exist
J_{2},J_{3}\geq J_{1}\geq J_{\textup{min}}\geq 1
and a uniform constant
C=C(m,M,J_{1},J_{2},J_{3},\eta)>0
such that, for any
|a|\leq M
and any solution

\bm{\mathfrak{S}}=(\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}],\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}],\widehat{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}],\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}])

to the modified Maxwell equations (
3.32
)–(
3.37
) arising from seed initial data supported on a fixed azimuthal mode
m
prescribed on
\Sigma_{0}
, the following energy estimates hold.

•

Degenerate energy boundedness: we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{J_{1}}\big[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](\tau)

\displaystyle\leq C\,{\mathbb{D}}_{\mathrm{bdd}}^{J_{2}}\big[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)\,,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{J_{1}}\big[r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](\tau)

\displaystyle\leq C\,{\mathbb{D}}_{\mathrm{bdd}}^{J_{2}}\big[r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)

for all
\tau\geq 0
.

•

Degenerate energy decay: we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{J_{1}}\big[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](\tau)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{(1+\tau)^{1-\eta}}\,\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{dec}}^{J_{3}}\big[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)\,,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{J_{1}}\big[r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](\tau)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{(1+\tau)^{1-\eta}}\,\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{dec}}^{J_{3}}\big[r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)

for all
\tau\geq 0
.

•

Decay at the event horizon: we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}\big[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](\tau,\infty)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{(1+\tau)^{2-\eta}}\,\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{dec}}^{J_{3}}\big[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)\,,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\left(|\not{\nabla}{\mathfrak{T}}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})|^{2}+|{\mathfrak{T}}(r^{2}\bm{\alpha})|^{2}\right)d\Omega

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{(1+\tau)^{1-\eta}}\,\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{dec}}^{J_{3}}\big[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)\,,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\left(|\not{\nabla}\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})|^{2}+|\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(r\bm{\underline{\alpha}})|^{2}\right)d\Omega

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{(1+\tau)^{1-\eta}}\,\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{dec}}^{J_{3}}\big[r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)\,,

for all
\tau\geq 0
.

In the above,
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{bdd}}^{J}
and
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{dec}}^{J}
are
J
-th order energy quantities depending only on the seed initial data. We also recall definition (
3.38
).

Remark 5.7
.

The energy norms appearing on the left hand side of the energy estimates in Conjecture
5.6
are degenerate at the event horizon (i.e. at
r=r_{+}
).

Remark 5.8
.

We note that Conjecture
5.6
is a uniform-in-
a
statement in the full
|a|\leq M
range. Very few such results exist in the literature at the present time. To the best of our knowledge, the only uniform-in-
a
statements in the full
|a|\leq M
range were shown in
[
34
]
for the radial ODE obtained from the Teukolsky equation (
3.27
) by (spacetime) frequency decomposition, but only in the case where the frequency parameters are sufficiently far from the so-called superradiant threshold. A lot of work in the physics and mathematics literature from recent years has focused on the
exactly
extremal
|a|=M
case. Conjecture
5.6
is formulated based on this literature, which we briefly review in Section
7.2
.

The second theorem proven in the present paper concerns energy boundedness and decay for the
middle
Maxwell components in the full
|a|\leq M
black hole parameter range and assumes Conjecture
5.6
. The energy estimates for the extremal Maxwell components of Conjecture
5.6
are embedded in the statement below and play a crucial role in the proof of the energy estimates for the middle Maxwell components.

Theorem 5.9

(Sub-extremal and extremal cases)
.

Fix
M>0
. Let
\eta\in(0,1)
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}
. Assume that Conjecture
5.6
holds. Then, there exist
J_{2},J_{3}\geq J_{1}\geq J_{\textup{min}}\geq 1
and a uniform constant
C=C(m,M,J_{1},J_{2},J_{3},\eta)>0
such that, for any
|a|\leq M
and any solution

\bm{\mathfrak{S}}=(\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}],\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}],\widehat{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}],\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}])

to the modified Maxwell equations (
3.32
)–(
3.37
) arising from seed initial data supported on a fixed azimuthal mode
m
prescribed on
\Sigma_{0}
, the following energy estimates hold.

•

Degenerate energy boundedness: we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{J_{1}}\big[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](\tau)

\displaystyle\leq C\,{\mathbb{D}}_{\mathrm{bdd}}^{J_{2}}\big[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)\,,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{J_{1}}\big[r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](\tau)

\displaystyle\leq C\,{\mathbb{D}}_{\mathrm{bdd}}^{J_{2}}\big[r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)\,,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{J_{1}}\big[\widehat{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](\tau)+\mathbb{E}^{J_{1}}\big[\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](\tau)

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\lVert|\widehat{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}})}+\lVert\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}})}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+C\left({\mathbb{D}}_{\mathrm{dec}}^{J_{3}}\big[r^{2}{\bm{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)+{\mathbb{D}}_{\mathrm{bdd}}^{J_{2}}\big[r^{2}{\bm{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+C\left({\mathbb{D}}_{\mathrm{dec}}^{J_{3}}\big[r{\bm{\underline{\alpha}}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)+{\mathbb{D}}_{\mathrm{bdd}}^{J_{2}}\big[r{\bm{\underline{\alpha}}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)\right)

for all
\tau\geq 0
.

•

Degenerate energy decay (extremal Maxwell components): we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{J_{1}}\big[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}]\big](\tau)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{(1+\tau)^{1-\eta}}\,\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{dec}}^{J_{3}}\big[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)\,,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{J_{1}}\big[r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}]\big](\tau)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{(1+\tau)^{1-\eta}}\,\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{dec}}^{J_{3}}\big[r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)

for all
\tau\geq 0
.

•

Degenerate energy decay (middle Maxwell components): there exist finite
\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, which can be read off from the seed initial data, such that the normalised solution
\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}=\bm{\mathfrak{S}}-\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\bm{\alpha}[\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}](\tau)

\displaystyle=\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}](\tau)\,,

\displaystyle\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}](\tau)

\displaystyle=\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}](\tau)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{J_{1}}\big[\widehat{\bm{\rho}}[\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}]\big](\tau)+\mathbb{E}^{J_{1}}\big[\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}\big[\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}]\big](\tau)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{(1+\tau)^{1-\eta}}\left({\mathbb{D}}_{\mathrm{dec}}^{J_{3}}\big[r^{2}{\bm{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)+{\mathbb{D}}_{\mathrm{bdd}}^{J_{2}}\big[r^{2}{\bm{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{C}{(1+\tau)^{1-\eta}}\left({\mathbb{D}}_{\mathrm{dec}}^{J_{3}}\big[r{\bm{\underline{\alpha}}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)+{\mathbb{D}}_{\mathrm{bdd}}^{J_{2}}\big[r{\bm{\underline{\alpha}}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\big](0)\right)

for all
\tau\geq 0
.

In the above,
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{bdd}}^{J}
and
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{dec}}^{J}
are the
J
-th order energy quantities introduced in Conjecture
5.6
.

Remark 5.10
.

As for Conjecture
5.6
, and contrary to Theorem
5.1
, the energy norms appearing on the left hand side of the energy estimates in Theorem
5.9
are degenerate at the event horizon (i.e. at
r=r_{+}
). Moreover, we note that Theorem
5.9
is a uniform-in-
a
statement in the full
|a|\leq M
range.

The proof of Theorems
5.1
and
5.9
is carried out in Section
8
and relies on the separate analysis of the middle and extremal Maxwell components, which is presented in Sections
6
and
7
respectively.

6
Analysis of the middle Maxwell components

In this section, we prove energy estimates for the middle Maxwell components in terms of energy norms of the extremal Maxwell components. The ones proven in this section are standalone statements. Once combined with the statements for the extremal Maxwell components of Section
7
, the energy estimates proven in this section establish energy boundedness and decay for the middle Maxwell components (hence the terminology “boundedness” and “decay” already appearing in Theorems
6.1
and
6.2
below and throughout the section). The energy estimates for the middle Maxwell components are obtained in a unified fashion for the full
|a|\leq M
range.

We state the first theorem of the section, which concerns non-degenerate-at-
\mathcal{H}^{+}
energy norms.

Theorem 6.1
.

Fix
M>0
. Let
J\geq 1
and
p_{\alpha}>0
. Assume that, for any
|a|\leq M
and any solution

\bm{\mathfrak{S}}=(\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}],\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}],\widehat{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}],\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}])

to the modified Maxwell equations (
3.32
)–(
3.37
) arising from seed initial data prescribed on
\Sigma_{0}
, the qualitative statement

\displaystyle\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}|\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}])|^{2}d\Omega=0

and the quantitative estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]](\tau,\infty)\leq\frac{\mathbb{D}[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]](0)}{(1+\tau)^{1+p_{\alpha}}}

hold, where
\mathbb{D}
is an energy quantity depending only on the seed initial data. Then, there exists a uniform constant
C=C(M,J,p_{\alpha})>0
such that the following energy estimates hold.

•

Non-degenerate energy boundedness: we have

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{J}[\widehat{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]](\tau)+\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{J}[\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]](\tau)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\lVert\widehat{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}}})}+\lVert\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}}})}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+C\left(\mathbb{D}[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]](0)+\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{\max\{J,1\}}[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]](\tau)+\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{\max\{J,1\}}[r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]](\tau)\right)

for all
\tau\geq 0
.

•

Non-degenerate energy decay: there exist finite
\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, which can be read off from the seed initial data, such that the normalised solution
\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}=\bm{\mathfrak{S}}-\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\bm{\alpha}[\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}](\tau)

\displaystyle=\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}](\tau)\,,

\displaystyle\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}](\tau)

\displaystyle=\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}](\tau)

and

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{J}[\widehat{\bm{\rho}}[\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}]](\tau)+\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{J}[\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}[\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}]](\tau)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\,\mathbb{D}[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]](0)}{(1+\tau)^{p_{\alpha}}}+C\left(\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{\max\{J,1\}}[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]](\tau)+\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{\max\{J,1\}}[r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]](\tau)\right)

for all
\tau\geq 0
.

We state the second theorem of the section, which concerns degenerate-at-
\mathcal{H}^{+}
energy norms.

Theorem 6.2
.

Fix
M>0
. Let
J\geq 1
and
p_{\alpha}>0
. Assume that, for any
|a|\leq M
and any solution

\bm{\mathfrak{S}}=(\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}],\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}],\widehat{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}],\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}])

to the modified Maxwell equations (
3.32
)–(
3.37
) arising from seed initial data prescribed on
\Sigma_{0}
, the qualitative statement

\displaystyle\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}|\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}])|^{2}d\Omega=0

and the quantitative estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]](\tau,\infty)\leq\frac{\mathbb{D}[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]](0)}{(1+\tau)^{1+p_{\alpha}}}

hold, where
\mathbb{D}
is an energy quantity depending only on the seed initial data. Then, there exists a uniform constant
C=C(M,J,p_{\alpha})>0
such that the following energy estimates hold.

•

Degenerate energy boundedness: we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{J}[\widehat{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]](\tau)+\mathbb{E}^{J}[\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]](\tau)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\lVert\widehat{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}}})}+\lVert\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}}})}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+C\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\left(|\not{\nabla}\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}])|^{2}+|\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}])|^{2}\right)d\Omega+C\,\mathbb{E}^{\max\{J,2\}}[r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]](\tau)

\displaystyle\qquad+C\,\mathbb{D}[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]](0)+C\,\mathbb{E}^{\max\{J,1\}}[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]](\tau)

for all
\tau\geq 0
.

•

Degenerate energy decay: there exist finite
\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, which can be read off from the seed initial data, such that the normalised solution
\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}=\bm{\mathfrak{S}}-\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\bm{\alpha}[\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}](\tau)

\displaystyle=\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}](\tau)\,,

\displaystyle\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}](\tau)

\displaystyle=\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}](\tau)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{J}[\widehat{\bm{\rho}}[\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}]](\tau)+\mathbb{E}^{J}[\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}[\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}]](\tau)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\left(|\not{\nabla}\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}])|^{2}+|\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}])|^{2}\right)d\Omega+C\,\mathbb{E}^{\max\{J,2\}}[r\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]](\tau)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{C\,\mathbb{D}[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]](0)}{(1+\tau)^{p_{\alpha}}}+C\,\mathbb{E}^{\max\{J,1\}}[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]](\tau)

for all
\tau\geq 0
.

Although both theorems are stated for the full
|a|\leq M
range, we note that, once combined with the statements for the extremal Maxwell components of Section
7
, Theorem
6.1
will lead to (non-degenerate) energy boundedness and decay for all Maxwell components in the sub-extremal
|a|<M
case (i.e. Theorem
5.1
), whereas Theorem
6.2
will lead to (degenerate) energy boundedness and decay for all Maxwell components in the full
|a|\leq M
black hole parameter range (i.e. Theorem
5.9
).

Sections
6.1
–
6.4
below contain the proof of Theorems
6.1
and
6.2
, which is divided as follows:

•

Section
6.1
: We prove estimates for the zeroth order energy flux (i.e.
L^{2}(\Sigma_{\tau})
-norm) of the middle Maxwell components. These estimates are achieved in Proposition
6.9
. To this end, one needs control over the zero mode of the middle Maxwell components (see Lemmas
6.3
and
6.6
), which is obtained by applying the
\mathbb{S}^{2}
-projection procedure of Section
2.4
and exploiting the enhanced structure of the algebraically special frame at the event horizon.

•

Section
6.2
: We establish estimates for first (and higher) order energy fluxes of the middle Maxwell components. These estimates are immediately read off from the Maxwell equations (
3.32
)–(
3.37
).

•

Section
6.3
: We combine the estimates of Sections
6.1
–
6.2
to prove Theorem
6.1
. The reader interested only in the sub-extremal
|a|<M
case need not read beyond this section.

•

Section
6.4
: We prove Theorem
6.2
by combining the estimates of Sections
6.1
–
6.2
with an additional pointwise identity (in the spin-weighted formalism) for transversal derivatives of the middle Maxwell components. As a warm-up, the additional identity is first derived in the Schwarzschild (and Reissner–Nordström) case (see Section
6.4.1
). The derivation of the identity in the Kerr case is carried out in a sequence of three lemmas in Section
6.4.2
(see the final Lemma
6.15
for a statement of the identity). In Section
6.4.3
, we employ the identity to prove Theorem
6.2
.

Additionally, we have:

•

Section
6.5
: Besides yielding a proof of Theorem
6.2
, the additional identity derived in Section
6.4
allows one to establish novel conservation laws along the event horizon for the middle Maxwell components under axisymmetry in the extremal
|a|=M
case.

6.1
Control over the zeroth order flux

By exploiting the Maxwell equations (
3.19
)–(
3.24
), we can derive conservation laws which control the zero mode of the middle Maxwell components
\Sigma{\bm{\rho}}
and
\Sigma{\bm{\sigma}}
along the event horizon, thereby obtaining the following lemma.

Lemma 6.3
.

Fix
M>0
. Then, there exists a uniform constant
C=C(M)>0
such that, for any
|a|\leq M
and any solution

\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}=(\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}],\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}],\bm{\rho}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}],\bm{\sigma}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}])

to the Maxwell equations (
3.19
)–(
3.24
) arising from seed initial data prescribed on
\Sigma_{0}
, the following estimates hold.

•

Horizon boundedness of zero modes: we have

\displaystyle\Bigg|\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\Sigma{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}]\,d\Omega\Bigg|^{2}+\Bigg|\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\Sigma{\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}]\,d\Omega\Bigg|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\left(\lVert\Sigma{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}]\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}}})}+\lVert\Sigma{\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}]\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}}})}+\lVert r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}]\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}}})}+\lVert r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}]\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}})}\right)

for all
\tau\geq 0
.

•

Horizon decay of zero modes: there exist finite
\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, which can be read off from the seed initial data, such that the normalised solution
\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}_{\circ}=\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}-\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}{}_{\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\Sigma{\bm{\rho}}[\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}_{\circ}]\,d\Omega\right|^{2}+\left|\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\Sigma{\bm{\sigma}}[\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}_{\circ}]\,d\Omega\right|^{2}\leq C\lVert r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}]\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}})}

for all
\tau\geq 0
.

Proof.

We start from the Maxwell equations (
3.21
)–(
3.22
). Restricted to
\mathcal{H}^{+}
, these take the form

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{4}(\Sigma\bm{\rho})

\displaystyle={\not{\textup{div}}}\,(\Sigma{\bm{\alpha}})\,,

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{4}(\Sigma\bm{\sigma})

\displaystyle=-{\not{\textup{curl}}}\,(\Sigma{\bm{\alpha}})\,.

(6.1)

We recall that, for
|a|\neq 0
, the differential operators on the right hand side of equations (
6.1
) are defined relative to the (non-integrable) distribution
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\rm as}}
. By applying the
\mathbb{S}^{2}
-projection formulae of Proposition
2.1
, we project equations (
6.1
) over the horizon foliation spheres
\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}
and obtain

\displaystyle\check{\not{\nabla}}_{4}\left(\Sigma\bm{\rho}-\frac{1}{2}\,\left(\mathfrak{k},\Sigma\widetilde{\bm{\alpha}}\right)_{\check{\not{g}}}\right)=\check{\not{\nabla}}_{4}(\Sigma\bm{\rho})-\frac{1}{2}\,\left(\mathfrak{k},\check{\not{\nabla}}_{4}\,(\Sigma\widetilde{\bm{\alpha}})\right)_{\check{\not{g}}}

\displaystyle=\check{\not{\textup{div}}}\,(\Sigma\widetilde{\bm{\alpha}})\,,

\displaystyle\check{\not{\nabla}}_{4}\left(\Sigma\bm{\sigma}+\frac{1}{2}\,\mathfrak{k}\wedge_{\check{\not{g}}}\Sigma\widetilde{\bm{\alpha}}\right)=\check{\not{\nabla}}_{4}(\Sigma\bm{\sigma})+\frac{1}{2}\,\mathfrak{k}\wedge_{\check{\not{g}}}\check{\not{\nabla}}_{4}\,(\Sigma\widetilde{\bm{\alpha}})

\displaystyle=-\check{\not{\textup{curl}}}\,(\Sigma\widetilde{\bm{\alpha}})\,,

where we used the identity
\check{\not{\nabla}}_{4}\mathfrak{k}|_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}=0
(see Proposition
2.1
). We note that the differential operators
\check{\not{\textup{div}}}
and
\check{\not{\textup{curl}}}
are now angular operators on
\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}
-spheres acting on
\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}
-tensors. One can therefore integrate over the
\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}
-spheres to obtain the conservation laws

\displaystyle\check{\not{\nabla}}_{4}\left[\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\left(\Sigma\bm{\rho}-\frac{1}{2}\,\left(\mathfrak{k},\Sigma\widetilde{\bm{\alpha}}\right)_{\check{\not{g}}}\right)\not{\varepsilon}_{\check{\not{g}}}\right]

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\check{\not{\nabla}}_{4}\left[\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\left(\Sigma\bm{\sigma}+\frac{1}{2}\,\mathfrak{k}\wedge_{\check{\not{g}}}\Sigma\widetilde{\bm{\alpha}}\right)\not{\varepsilon}_{\check{\not{g}}}\right]

\displaystyle=0

(6.2)

or, equivalently, the identities

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\Sigma\bm{\rho}\,\not{\varepsilon}_{\check{\not{g}}}&=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}}}\left(\Sigma\bm{\rho}-\frac{1}{2}\,\left(\mathfrak{k},\Sigma\widetilde{\bm{\alpha}}\right)_{\check{\not{g}}}\right)\not{\varepsilon}_{\check{\not{g}}}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\left(\mathfrak{k},\Sigma\widetilde{\bm{\alpha}}\right)_{\check{\not{g}}}\not{\varepsilon}_{\check{\not{g}}}\,,\\
\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\Sigma\bm{\sigma}\,\not{\varepsilon}_{\check{\not{g}}}&=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}}}\left(\Sigma\bm{\sigma}+\frac{1}{2}\,\mathfrak{k}\wedge_{\check{\not{g}}}\Sigma\widetilde{\bm{\alpha}}\right)\not{\varepsilon}_{\check{\not{g}}}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\left(\mathfrak{k}\wedge_{\check{\not{g}}}\Sigma\widetilde{\bm{\alpha}}\right)\not{\varepsilon}_{\check{\not{g}}}\,.\end{split}

(6.3)

By using the identity (
2.32
), we can replace
\not{\varepsilon}_{\check{\not{g}}}
by
d\Omega_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}
in identities (
6.2
)–(
6.3
). We can then estimate the last term of each of the identities (
6.3
) as follows

\displaystyle\Bigg|\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\left(\mathfrak{k},\Sigma\widetilde{\bm{\alpha}}\right)_{\check{\not{g}}}d\Omega\Bigg|^{2}+\Bigg|\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\left(\mathfrak{k}\wedge_{\check{\not{g}}}\Sigma\widetilde{\bm{\alpha}}\right)d\Omega\Bigg|^{2}\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}|\widetilde{\bm{\alpha}}|_{\check{\not{g}}}^{2}\,d\Omega=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}|{\bm{\alpha}}|^{2}_{\not{g}}\,d\Omega\,,

where we estimated
\sup_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}|\mathfrak{k}|_{\check{\not{g}}}\lesssim 1
depending only on the spacetime parameters and used the property (
2.30
) in the last equality. On the other hand, the first term on the right hand side of identities (
6.3
) is determined by initial data. In particular, by setting

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\mathfrak{q}_{1}:=\frac{1}{I_{a,M}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}}}\left(\Sigma\bm{\rho}-\frac{1}{2}\,\left(\mathfrak{k},\Sigma\widetilde{\bm{\alpha}}\right)_{\check{\not{g}}}\right)d\Omega\,,\qquad\mathfrak{q}_{2}:=\frac{1}{I_{a,M}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}}}\left(\Sigma\bm{\sigma}+\frac{1}{2}\,\mathfrak{k}\wedge_{\check{\not{g}}}\Sigma\widetilde{\bm{\alpha}}\right)d\Omega\,,\end{gathered}

(6.4)

\displaystyle I_{a,M}:=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}}}\frac{1-(a/r_{+})^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}{1+(a/r_{+})^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}\,d\Omega=4\pi\left(\frac{2\arctan(a/r_{+})}{(a/r_{+})}-1\right),

those terms vanish for the renormalised solution
\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}_{\circ}=\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}-\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ}{}_{\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}}
.
∎

Remark 6.4
.

By expressing the Maxwell tensor
\bm{F}
in terms of
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\underline{\alpha}},\bm{\rho},\bm{\sigma})
, one can check that the conserved (along
\mathcal{H}^{+}
) quantities in (
6.2
) correspond to the (everywhere) conserved topological charges

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r}}\bm{F}\,,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r}}\bm{\star}\bm{F}

(6.5)

when the latter integrals are computed at
\mathcal{H}^{+}
.

Remark 6.5
.

For
|a|=0
, it follows from equations (
3.21
)–(
3.22
) that one has the conservation laws

\displaystyle{\not{\nabla}}_{4}\left[\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r}}\Sigma\bm{\rho}\,d\Omega\right]

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle{\not{\nabla}}_{4}\left[\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r}}\Sigma\bm{\sigma}\,d\Omega\right]

\displaystyle=0

for
any

r\in[2M,\infty)
. Indeed, the differential operators on the right hand side of equations (
3.21
)–(
3.22
) are now angular operators on the foliation spheres, thus giving rise to vanishing integrals over the spheres.

The Maxwell equations (
3.19
)–(
3.24
) are
coupled
equations for derivatives of
\Sigma\bm{\rho}
and
\Sigma\bm{\sigma}
. For the remainder of the analysis, it will be more convenient to work with the modified Maxwell equations (
3.32
)–(
3.37
), which are
decoupled
equations for derivatives of
\widehat{\bm{\rho}}
and
\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}
. To this end, we next show that the previous Lemma
6.3
yields control over the zero mode of the modified middle Maxwell components
\widehat{\bm{\rho}}
and
\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}
along the event horizon.

Lemma 6.6
.

Fix
M>0
and let
p_{\alpha}>0
. Assume that, for any
|a|\leq M
and any solution

\bm{\mathfrak{S}}=(\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}],\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}],\widehat{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}],\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}])

to the modified Maxwell equations (
3.32
)–(
3.37
) arising from seed initial data prescribed on
\Sigma_{0}
, the qualitative statement

\displaystyle\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\left(|\mathfrak{T}(\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}])|^{2}+|\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(\bm{\underline{\alpha}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}])|^{2}\right)d\Omega=0

and the quantitative estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]](\tau,\infty)\leq\frac{\mathbb{D}[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]](0)}{(1+\tau)^{1+p_{\alpha}}}

hold, where
\mathbb{D}
is an energy quantity depending only on the seed initial data. Then, there exists a uniform constant
C=C(M,p_{\alpha})>0
such that the following estimates hold.

•

Horizon boundedness of zero modes: we have

\displaystyle\Bigg|\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\widehat{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\,d\Omega\Bigg|^{2}+\Bigg|\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\,d\Omega\Bigg|^{2}\leq C\left(\lVert\widehat{\bm{\rho}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}}})}+\lVert\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}}})}+\mathbb{D}[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]](0)\right)

for all
\tau\geq 0
.

•

Horizon decay of zero modes: there exist finite
\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, which can be read off from the seed initial data, such that the normalised solution
\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}=\bm{\mathfrak{S}}-\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\widehat{\bm{\rho}}[\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}]\,d\Omega\right|^{2}+\left|\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}[\widetilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}]\,d\Omega\right|^{2}\leq C\frac{\mathbb{D}[r^{2}\bm{\alpha}[\bm{\mathfrak{S}}]](0)}{(1+\tau)^{p_{\alpha}}}

for all
\tau\geq 0
.

Proof.

Let
\gamma:=\frac{a}{r_{+}}
. We note

\displaystyle I_{\gamma}

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\frac{1-\gamma^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}{1+\gamma^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}\,d\Omega=4\pi\left(\frac{2\arctan\gamma}{\gamma}-1\right)\neq 0\,,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\frac{\cos\theta\,d\Omega}{1+\gamma^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}

\displaystyle=0\,.

Let
f_{\rho},f_{\sigma}\colon[0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle f_{\rho}(\tau)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\widehat{\bm{\rho}}\,d\Omega\,,

\displaystyle f_{\sigma}(\tau):=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}\,d\Omega\,.

By the fundamental theorem of calculus and equation (
3.35
), we have

\displaystyle|f_{\rho}(\tau)-f_{\rho}(0)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau^{\prime},r_{+}}}\not{\nabla}_{4}\widehat{\bm{\rho}}\,d\Omega d\tau^{\prime}\right|\lesssim\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau^{\prime},r_{+}}}(|\not{\nabla}\bm{\alpha}|+|\bm{\alpha}|)\,d\Omega d\tau^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}[\bm{\alpha}](0,1)}+\int_{1}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau^{\prime},r_{+}}}(|\not{\nabla}\bm{\alpha}|+|\bm{\alpha}|)\,d\Omega d\tau^{\prime}\,.

(6.6)

To control the second term in (
6.6
), we consider the dyadic sequence
\{\tau_{i}=2^{i}\}_{i=0}^{\infty}
and obtain

\displaystyle\int_{1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\left(|{\bm{\alpha}}|+|\not{\nabla}{\bm{\alpha}}|\right)d\Omega d\tau

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\left(|{\bm{\alpha}}|+|\not{\nabla}{\bm{\alpha}}|\right)d\Omega d\tau

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\tau_{i+1}-\tau_{i}}\Big(\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\left(|{\bm{\alpha}}|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}{\bm{\alpha}}|^{2}\right)d\Omega d\tau\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\tau_{i}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}[{\bm{\alpha}}](\tau_{i},\infty)\right)^{1/2}\lesssim\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\tau_{i}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathbb{D}[r^{2}{\bm{\alpha}}](0)}{\tau_{i}^{1+p_{\alpha}}}}\lesssim\sqrt{\mathbb{D}[r^{2}{\bm{\alpha}}](0)}\,.

(6.7)

Combining (
6.7
) with (
6.6
), we deduce

\displaystyle|f_{\rho}(\tau)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert\widehat{\bm{\rho}}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}})}+\sqrt{\mathbb{D}[r^{2}{\bm{\alpha}}](0)}\,,

\displaystyle|f_{\sigma}(\tau)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{0,r_{+}})}+\sqrt{\mathbb{D}[r^{2}{\bm{\alpha}}](0)}\,,

where the latter estimate is derived by an analogous argument. This concludes the proof of the first bullet point in the lemma. For the second bullet point in the lemma, we rely on the previous Lemma
6.3
. In view of (
3.29
)–(
3.30
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\widehat{\bm{\rho}}\,d\Omega

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\Sigma\bm{\rho}\,d\Omega

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\frac{1-\gamma^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}{1+\gamma^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}(\widehat{\bm{\rho}}-f_{\rho})\,d\Omega+\frac{2\gamma}{I_{\gamma}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\frac{\cos\theta}{1+\gamma^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}(\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}-f_{\sigma})\,d\Omega\,,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}\,d\Omega

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\Sigma\bm{\sigma}\,d\Omega

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\frac{1-\gamma^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}{1+\gamma^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}(\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}-f_{\sigma})\,d\Omega-\frac{2\gamma}{I_{\gamma}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\frac{\cos\theta}{1+\gamma^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}(\widehat{\bm{\rho}}-f_{\rho})\,d\Omega\,.

By using equations (
3.37
)–(
3.36
), we can estimate the last two terms on the right hand side by

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\frac{1-\gamma^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}{1+\cos^{2}\theta}(\widehat{\bm{\rho}}-f_{\rho})\,d\Omega\Big|^{2}+\Big|\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\frac{\cos\theta}{1+\gamma^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}(\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}-f_{\sigma})\,d\Omega\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\Big|\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\frac{1-\gamma^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}{1+\cos^{2}\theta}(\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}-f_{\sigma})\,d\Omega\Big|^{2}+\Big|\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\frac{\cos\theta}{1+\gamma^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}(\widehat{\bm{\rho}}-f_{\rho})\,d\Omega\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\left[(\widehat{\bm{\rho}}-f_{\rho})^{2}+(\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}-f_{\sigma})^{2}\right]\,d\Omega\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\left(|{\not{\nabla}}\widehat{\bm{\rho}}|^{2}+|{\not{\nabla}}\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}|^{2}\right)d\Omega\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\left(|\mathfrak{T}(\bm{\alpha})|^{2}+|\underline{\mathfrak{T}}(\bm{\underline{\alpha}})|^{2}\right)d\Omega\,.

By the assumptions on the dynamics of
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\underline{\alpha}})
in the statement of the lemma, we deduce that

\displaystyle\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\widehat{\bm{\rho}}\,d\Omega=\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\Sigma\bm{\rho}\,d\Omega\,,\qquad\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}\,d\Omega=\frac{1}{I_{\gamma}}\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\Sigma\bm{\sigma}\,d\Omega\,.

(6.8)

By Lemma
6.3
, we deduce
\tau
-decay (without a rate) of
f_{\rho}
and
f_{\sigma}
for the normalised solution
\tilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}
, i.e.
\lim_{\tau\to\infty}f_{\rho}(\tau)=\lim_{\tau\to\infty}f_{\sigma}(\tau)=0
. By the fundamental theorem of calculus, the normalised solution
\tilde{\bm{\mathfrak{S}}}
satisfies

\displaystyle|f_{\rho}(\tau)|\lesssim\Bigg|\int_{\tau}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau^{\prime},r_{+}}}\not{\nabla}_{4}\widehat{\bm{\rho}}\,d\Omega d\tau^{\prime}\Bigg|\,,\qquad|f_{\sigma}(\tau)|\lesssim\Bigg|\int_{\tau}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau^{\prime},r_{+}}}\not{\nabla}_{4}\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}\,d\Omega d\tau^{\prime}\Bigg|\,.

By applying the same strategy as in the boundedness argument, one considers the sequence
\left\{\tau_{i}=2^{i}\tau_{0}\right\}_{i=0}^{\infty}
with
\tau_{0}=1+\tau
and uses the assumptions on the dynamics of
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\underline{\alpha}})
in the statement of the lemma to obtain

\displaystyle|f_{\rho}(\tau)|^{2}+|f_{\sigma}(\tau)|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}[{\bm{\alpha}}](\tau,\tau_{0})+\left(\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\tau_{i}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathbb{D}[r^{2}{\bm{\alpha}}](0)}{\tau_{i}^{1+p_{\alpha}}}}\right)^{2}\lesssim\frac{\mathbb{D}[r^{2}{\bm{\alpha}}](0)}{(1+\tau)^{p_{\alpha}}}\,,

which concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Remark 6.7
.

In the proof of Lemma
6.6
, we obtain the boundedness part of the statement by relying solely on the modified Maxwell equations. In contrast, the decay argument relies on the prior choice of charges and decay estimates of Lemma
6.3
for the original middle Maxwell components
\Sigma\bm{\rho}
and
\Sigma\bm{\sigma}
. However, we point out that, once the boundedness part of Lemma
6.6
is established, one may choose finite charges on the
final
horizon sphere such that

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{q}}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\widehat{\bm{\rho}}\,d\Omega\,,

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}\,d\Omega

(6.9)

and directly prove the decay part of Lemma
6.6
for the modified middle Maxwell components
\widehat{\bm{\rho}}
and
\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}
by subtracting the modified stationary solution
\bm{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}}
. This alternative decay argument would only employ the modified Maxwell equations (
3.34
)–(
3.35
), thus producing a full proof of Lemma
6.6
which never invokes Lemma
6.3
nor the original system of Maxwell equations. The teleological choice of charges (
6.9
) and alternative decay argument would moreover allow one to dispense with any projection procedure over spheres. Note, however, that, in view of (
6.8
), (
6.2
) and (
6.4
), invoking the projection procedure over spheres
a posteriori
would allow us to deduce that the teleological charges can be read off from the initial data: in other words, the choices (
6.4
) and (
6.9
) turn out to be the same.

Remark 6.8
.

Once combined with the modified Maxwell equations for derivatives of
\widehat{\bm{\rho}}
and
\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}
, Lemma
6.6
immediately provides a-priori control over the
L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\tau,r})
-norm of
\widehat{\bm{\rho}}
and
\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}
along the event horizon.

Lemma
6.6
can be applied to establish control over the
L^{2}(\Sigma_{\tau})
-norm of the modified middle Maxwell components
\widehat{\bm{\rho}}
and
\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}
, as stated in the following proposition. We remark that, in the estimates stated in the proposition, all future energy norms of the extremal Maxwell components appearing on the right hand side are degenerate at
\mathcal{H}^{+}
. We also note that the estimates possess near-horizon integrals of transversal derivatives of
\widehat{\bm{\rho}}
and
\widehat{\bm{\sigma}}
